\displaystyle\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H}}\cdot g\cdot h\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H}}\cdot g\cdot h}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right](\omega)

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{H}}\hat{g}(\eta,X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)\cdot\hat{h}(\eta,X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)\,\mu_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}\circ\pi_{H}^{-1}(\,\text{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{\Lambda_{H}^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in A_{H}}\cdot\hat{g}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)\cdot\hat{h}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\cdot Z_{S^{c}\cap H^{c}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{\Lambda_{H}^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in A_{H}}\cdot\hat{g}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)\cdot\hat{h}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\cdot Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}.

(9)

We proceed by simplifying (
9
).
Observe that

\displaystyle\hat{h}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}\cap S)

\displaystyle=C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},X_{t}\cap S}}\right)\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap S))\cap\partial B}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap S))\cap(\partial B\cup\boldsymbol{v})}}

\displaystyle=C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},X_{t}\cap S}}\right)\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap S))\cap B^{c}}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap S))\cap(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v})^{c}}}

\displaystyle=C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},X_{t}\cap S}}\right)\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap S)}}.

Here, the second equality follows from
Lemma

C.2
and the fact that, for all
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
, it holds that the distance between
\Lambda_{B}^{c}\setminus\Lambda_{\partial B}
and
\Lambda_{B}
, and the distance between
\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{c}\setminus\Lambda_{\partial B\cup\boldsymbol{v}}
and
\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}
are at least
r
(the range of the potential).
Moreover, the last equality follows from the fact that
S\cap B^{c}=S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}=R
,
\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\subseteq\Lambda_{H}\subseteq\Lambda_{B}^{c}\subseteq\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{c}
and
S\subseteq(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v})^{c}
.
Further, note that

\displaystyle\hat{g}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}\cap S)

\displaystyle=\mu_{B}^{(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap S))\cap B^{c}}(A_{B})=\mu_{B}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)}(A_{B})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{1}{m!}\int_{\Lambda_{B}^{m}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{y}}\in A_{B}}\cdot(\lambda_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)})^{\boldsymbol{y}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{y}}{Z_{B}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)}}.

Substituting both back into (
9
) and canceling
Z_{B}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)}
and
Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap S)}
yields

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H},X_{t+1}\cap B\in A_{B},F_{t}=1\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H},X_{t+1}\cap B\in A_{B},F_{t}=1}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt=\frac{C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},X_{t}\cap S}}\right)}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap S}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{\Lambda_{H}^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in A_{H}}\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}\cap S})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{1}{m!}\int_{\Lambda_{B}^{m}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{y}}\in A_{B}}\cdot(\lambda_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)})^{\boldsymbol{y}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right]\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}.

Moreover, note that
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}},\Lambda_{H}}}\right)\geq r
.
Thus, it holds for
n\in\mathds{N}_{0}
and
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in\Lambda_{H}^{n}
that

\displaystyle(\lambda_{X_{t}\cap S})^{\boldsymbol{x}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{z\in X_{t}\cap S}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},z}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{z\in X_{t}\cap R}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},z}}\right)}

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{X_{t}\cap R})^{\boldsymbol{x}}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H},X_{t+1}\cap B\in A_{B},F_{t}=1\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H},X_{t+1}\cap B\in A_{B},F_{t}=1}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},X_{t}\cap S}}\right)}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap S}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{\Lambda_{H}^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in A_{H}}\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}\cap R})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{1}{m!}\int_{\Lambda_{B}^{m}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{y}}\in A_{B}}\cdot(\lambda_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)})^{\boldsymbol{y}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right]\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}.

Next, note that, since
H\cup B=(R)^{\mathrm{c}}
, we it holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{\Lambda_{H}^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in A_{H}}\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}\cap R})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{1}{m!}\int_{\Lambda_{B}^{m}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{y}}\in A_{B}}(\lambda_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}\cap R)})^{\boldsymbol{y}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right]\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\quad\quad=Z_{R^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap R}\cdot\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap R}(\pi_{H}^{-1}(A_{H})\cap\pi_{B}^{-1}(A_{B}))

This finally yields the expression

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H},X_{t+1}\cap B\in A_{B},F_{t}=1\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap H\in A_{H},X_{t+1}\cap B\in A_{B},F_{t}=1}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad=C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},X_{t}\cap S}}\right)\cdot\frac{Z_{R^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap R}}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap S}}\cdot\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap R}(\pi_{H}^{-1}(A_{H})\cap\pi_{B}^{-1}(A_{B}))

∎

We now continue with our main argument.
The next lemma is the counterpart of
Lemma

5.5
for the case
F_{t}=0
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

5.4
, let
E_{0}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t}=S,\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v},F_{t}=0}}\right\}
for some
S\in 2^{\mathcal{V}}\setminus\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\emptyset}}\right\}
and
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
such that
\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{E_{0}}}}\right]>0
, and set
B=B\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\ell}}\right)
and
R=S\cup\partial B
.
For all
A\in\mathfrak{R}

it holds that

\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t+1}\cap R;E_{0}}}\right.}}}\right]=\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t+1}\cap R}(A).

Proof.

An example that might help to keep track of the relevant regions throughout this proof is given in
Figure

3
.

Figure 3
:
Example for the regions considered throughout the proof of
Lemma

5.8
for
\ell=2
.

Let
E=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t}=S,\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v}}}\right\}
for
S
and
\boldsymbol{v}
as in the definition of
E_{0}
. Since
X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in\mathcal{N}_{R^{c}}
and
\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t+1}\cap R}
is only supported on
\mathcal{N}_{R^{c}}
, it again suffices to prove the statement for
A\in\mathfrak{R}_{R^{c}}
.
Our first step is to show that the following claim

Claim 5.9
.

It holds that

\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t}\cap R;E}}\right.}}}\right]=\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap R}(A)

Given
5.9
, we aim for changing the probability measure from
\mathds{P}_{E}
to
\mathds{P}_{E_{0}}
.
To achieve this, note that by definition
F_{t}
does only depend on
X_{t}
via
X_{t}\cap R
.
Thus, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{F_{t}=0\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{F_{t}=0}X_{t}\cap R;E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{F_{t}=0\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{F_{t}=0}X_{t}\cap R,X_{t}\cap R^{c};E}}\right.}}}\right].

Therefore,
Lemma

A.3
implies

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t}\cap R;E_{0}}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t}\cap R;E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap R}(A).

Finally, observe that, given
E_{0}
, it holds that
X_{t+1}=X_{t}
.
Thus, we have

\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t+1}\cap R;E_{0}}}\right.}}}\right]=\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t+1}\cap R}(A)

as desired.
∎

Before using
Lemmas

5.5
and
5.8
to show
Lemma

5.3
, we first prove
5.9
.

Proof of
5.9
.

Define
f:\Omega\to[0,1],\omega\mapsto\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap R}(A)
.
Since
f
is
\sigma(X_{t}\cap R)
-measurable, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R\in D}\cdot f\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R\in D}\cdot f}E}}\right.}}}\right]=\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R\in D}\cdot\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R\in D}\cdot\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}}E}}\right.}}}\right]

(10)

for all events
D\in\mathfrak{R}_{R}
.
Further, since
R
is the union of the disjoint sets
S
and
\partial B\setminus S
,
Lemma

C.1
allows us to focus on events of the form
\{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1},X_{t}\cap S\in D_{2}\}
for
D_{1}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\partial B\setminus S}
and
D_{2}\in\mathfrak{R}_{S}
.
Writing

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap S\in D_{2}}\cdot f\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap S\in D_{2}}\cdot f}E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap S\in D_{2}}\cdot\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right]\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap S\in D_{2}}\cdot\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right]}E}}\right.}}}\right]

shows that, for proving (
10
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right].

To this end, define
\hat{f}:\mathcal{N}_{\partial B\setminus S}\times\mathcal{N}_{S}\to[0,1]
by

\hat{f}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})=\mu_{R^{c}}^{\eta_{1}\cup\eta_{2}}(A)

and note that
\hat{f}(X_{t}(\omega)\cap(\partial B\setminus S),X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)=f(\omega)
for all
\omega\in\Omega
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot f}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S),X_{t}\cap S)\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S),X_{t}\cap S)}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right].

We proceed by deriving an explicit expression for the right-hand side.
By
Lemma

5.4
and the assumption of the lemma, we know that

(\omega,\cdot)\mapsto\mu_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}\circ\pi_{\partial B\setminus S}^{-1}(\cdot)

is regular conditional distribution for
X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)
given
X_{t}\cap S
under
\mathds{P}_{E}
.
Thus, using
Theorem

A.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S),X_{t}\cap S)\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S),X_{t}\cap S)}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right](\omega)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{D_{1}}\hat{f}(\eta,X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)\,\mu_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}\circ\pi_{\partial B\setminus S}^{-1}(\,\text{d}\eta)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{(\Lambda_{\partial B\setminus S})^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\cdot Z_{(\partial B\setminus S)^{c}\cap S^{c}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{(\Lambda_{\partial B\setminus S})^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\cdot Z_{R^{c}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

for
\mathds{P}_{E}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega
.
Further, note that

\displaystyle\hat{f}(\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}},X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\mu_{R^{c}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\frac{1}{Z_{R^{c}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)}}\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{1}{m!}\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{R}^{c}}}\right)^{m}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{y}}\in A}\cdot(\lambda_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)})^{\boldsymbol{y}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{y}.

Thus, after canceling
Z_{R^{c}}^{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S),X_{t}\cap S)\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\mathds{1}_{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1}}\cdot\hat{f}(X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S),X_{t}\cap S)}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right](\omega)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{Z_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{(\Lambda_{\partial B\setminus S})^{n}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in D_{1}}\cdot(\lambda_{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S})^{\boldsymbol{x}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{1}{m!}\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{R}^{c}}}\right)^{m}}\mathds{1}_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{y}}\in A}\cdot(\lambda_{\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}\cup(X_{t}(\omega)\cap S)})^{\boldsymbol{y}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)}\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right]\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}(\omega)\cap S}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{\partial B\setminus S}^{-1}(D_{1})\cap\pi_{R^{c}}^{-1}(A)}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1},X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap(\partial B\setminus S)\in D_{1},X_{t}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t}\cap S;E}}\right.}}}\right](\omega)

for
\mathds{P}_{E}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega
, where the last equality is once again due to
Lemma

5.4
.
This shows (
10
) and consequently proves the claim.
∎

We proceed to show
Lemma

5.3
by combining
Lemmas

5.5
and
5.8
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.3
.

First, note that is suffices to show that, for all
t\in\mathds{N}_{0}
and
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
with
\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{U}_{t}=S}}}\right]>0
, it holds that
\mu_{S^{c}}^{X_{t}\cap S}(A)
is a version of the conditional expectation
\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t}\cap S^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t}\cap S^{c}\in A}X_{t}\cap S,\mathcal{U}_{t}=S}}\right.}}}\right]
.
The second part of the statement then follows as
\mu_{S^{c}}^{\eta}
is a probability distribution for all
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{S}
.

We proceed by proving our claim by an induction over
t
.
For
t=0
, the statement is trivially true since
\mathcal{U}_{0}=\mathcal{V}
with probability
1
.

Now, assume the lemma holds for some fixed
t\in N
and let
R\subseteq\mathcal{V}
be such that
\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{U}_{t+1}=R}}}\right]>0
.
We start with the case
R\neq\emptyset
.
We consider two sets of event
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{1}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{i}
for
i\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}
consists of all events of the form
C=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t}=S,\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v},F_{t}=i}}\right\}
with
S\in 2^{\mathcal{V}}\setminus\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\emptyset}}\right\}
and
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
such that
\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{C\cap\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t+1}=R}}\right\}}}}\right]>0
.
Set
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{0}\cup\mathcal{C}_{1}
and note that all events in
\mathcal{C}
are pairwise disjoint.
Moreover, it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\bigcup\nolimits_{C\in\mathcal{C}}C\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{\bigcup\nolimits_{C\in\mathcal{C}}C}\mathcal{U}_{t+1}=R}}\right.}}}\right]=1.

Thus, given we show that

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t}\cap R;C}}\right.}}}\right]=\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t+1}\cap R}(A)

(11)

for all
C\in\mathcal{C}
, then
Lemma

A.2
implies that

\mu_{R^{c}}^{X_{t+1}\cap R}(A)
is also a version of the conditional expectation
\mathds{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{X_{t+1}\cap R^{c}\in A}X_{t}\cap R;\mathcal{U}_{t+1}=R}}\right.}}}\right]
as desired.
Suppose that
C\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
Then
C
must have the form
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t}=S,\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v},F_{t}=1}}\right\}
for some
S
and
\boldsymbol{v}
with
R=S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}
.
Thus, using the induction hypothesis and applying
Lemma

5.5
proves (
11
).
Otherwise, if
C\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, then
C
is of the form
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t}=S,\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v},F_{t}=0}}\right\}
with
R=S\cup\partial B\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\ell}}\right)
.
Using the induction hypothesis and
Lemma

5.8
shows (
11
).

It remains to consider the case
R=\emptyset
.
We construct
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{1}
as before, but we set
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{0}\cup\mathcal{C}_{1}\cup\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t}=\emptyset}}\right\}}}\right\}
.
Again, by
Lemma

A.2
it suffices to argue (
11
) for all
C\in\mathcal{C}
.
The cases
C\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
C\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
are handled as before and we focus on
C=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{U}_{t}=\emptyset}}\right\}
.
By our definition of the process, we fixed
X_{t}
and
\mathcal{U}_{t}
to remain constant once
\mathcal{U}_{t}=\emptyset
.
Thus, (
11
) follows directly from the induction hypothesis, concluding the proof.
∎

6
Strong spatial mixing and success probabilities of Bayes filters

Recall that for
Algorithm

1
to terminate rapidly, we need to ensure that the success probability of the Bayes filter is close to
1
.
In this section, we prove a general statement that allows us to control the success probabilities of the Bayes filters we will construct in the upcoming sections.
Readers only interested in the actual construction of the Bayes filter may skip this section for now and return to it later for the running time analysis.

The main technical lemma of this section states that, under strong spatial mixing, a certain fraction of partition functions that is central for the construction of our Bayes filters can be brought arbitrarily close to
1
by increasing the update radius
\ell
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
be non-empty,
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
,
\ell\in\mathds{N}
, and set
B=B\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\ell}}\right)
and
H=(S\cup B)^{\mathrm{c}}
.
Suppose
\phi
exhibits
(a,b)
-strong spatial mixing up to
\lambda
.
Then, for all feasible
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
and all
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathcal{N}_{H}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({-a3^{d}r^{d}\mathrm{e}^{2br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda r^{d}+\mathrm{e}^{\lambda 3^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-br\ell}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({a3^{d}r^{d}\mathrm{e}^{2br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda r^{d}+\mathrm{e}^{\lambda 3^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-br\ell}}}}\right).

Before we prove
Lemma

6.1
, we briefly sketch how it helps control the success probability of the Bayes filter. Recall
Definition

4.1
and assume we would directly use

\displaystyle C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta}}\right)=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}\\
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)\text{ is feasible}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}}}\right\}

(12)

as Bayes filter correction.
Assuming
X_{t}=\eta
,
\mathcal{U}_{t}=S
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v}\in S
,
Lemma

C.2
yields that the probability that
F_{t}=1
is

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}\\
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)\text{ is feasible}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}}}\right\}\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{\eta\cap\partial B}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\eta\cap(\partial B\cup\boldsymbol{v})}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}\\
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)\text{ is feasible}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{(\eta\cap H)\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{(\eta\cap H)\cup(\eta\cap S)}}}}\right\}.

Applying
Lemma

6.1
with
\xi_{1}=\xi
and
\xi_{2}=\eta\cap H
allows us to lower bound the probability that
F_{t}=1
by
\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({-a3^{d}r^{d}\mathrm{e}^{2br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda r^{d}+\mathrm{e}^{\lambda 3^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-br\ell}}}}\right)
.
Thus, by increasing the update radius
\ell
we could bring the success probability of the Bayes filter arbitrary close to
1
.
While we will not use exactly (
12
) as Bayes filter correction, we can apply
Lemma

6.1
in a similar fashion when using a suitable approximation.
More on that in
Section

7
and
Section

8
.

To prove
Lemma

6.1
, we first show that strong spatial mixing implies correlation decay in terms of
k
-point density functions.
The converse of this statement was previously shown in
[
58
]
.
We then use an identity from
[
58
,
56
]
to derive our lemma.

6.1
Strong spatial mixing and point density functions

Let
\Lambda\subset\mathds{R}^{d}
be a bounded measurable region and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
be an activity function.
For every
k\in\mathds{N}
the
k
-point density of the Gibbs point process
\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda},\Lambda}
at
\boldsymbol{x}\in\Lambda^{k}
is defined as

\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)},

where
\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}}
denotes the activity function
y\mapsto\boldsymbol{\lambda}(y)\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i\in[k]}\phi(y,x_{i})}
.

Recall
Definition

1.1
and note that, for a measurable space
(\Omega,\mathcal{A})
and probability measures
P
and
Q
, an equivalent definition of total variation distance is

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert P-Q}}\right\rvert_{TV}=\sup_{f:\Omega\to[-1,1]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathds{E}_{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f}}}\right]-\mathds{E}_{Q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f}}}\right]}}\right|,

where the supremum is taken over measurable functions.
Using this definition we obtain the following statement.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\phi
be a repulsive pair potential of range
r
and let
\lambda,a,b\in\mathds{R}_{>0}
such that
\phi
exhibits
(a,b)
-strong spatial mixing up to
\lambda
.
Let
\Lambda\subset\mathds{R}^{d}
be bounded and measurable, and consider activity functions
\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}<\lambda
.
For all measurable
{\Lambda^{\prime}}\subseteq\Lambda
disjoint from
\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)
, all
k\in\mathds{N}
and all
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{k}
it holds that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)-\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}\right|\leq\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}a\mathrm{e}^{br}\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)},

where
{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{y\in\Lambda\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{y\in\Lambda}\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y,{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}\right)<r}}\right.}}\right\}
, and
\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
and
\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}
are the
k
-point densities of
\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda},\Lambda}
and
\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}
.

Proof.

For every
k\in\mathds{N}
and
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\in{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{k}
define
f_{\boldsymbol{x}}:\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}\to[0,1]
by

f_{\boldsymbol{x}}(\eta)=\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{y\in\eta}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},y}}\right)}.

By definition, it holds that

\displaystyle\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathds{E}_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda},\Lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f_{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}\right].

Since the range of
\phi
is bounded by
r
, it holds that
f_{\boldsymbol{x}}
is local on
{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{y\in\Lambda\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{y\in\Lambda}\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y,{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}\right)<r}}\right.}}\right\}
.
Applying the same reasoning to
\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)
and observing that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}
on
{\Lambda^{\prime}}
yields

\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathds{E}_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f_{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}\right].

Next, note that for every
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
it holds that
f_{\boldsymbol{x}}(\eta)=f_{\boldsymbol{x}}\circ\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}(\eta)
, where
\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}
is the projection
\eta\mapsto\eta\cap{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}
.
Using the change-of-variables formula for Lebesgue integration, we get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)-\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}\right|

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathds{E}_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda},\Lambda}\circ\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}^{-1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f_{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}\right]-\mathds{E}_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}\circ\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}^{-1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f_{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}\right]}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda},\Lambda}-\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}}}\right\rvert_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}},

where the inequality follows from the definition of the total variation distance given above and the fact that
f_{\boldsymbol{x}}
has domain
[0,1]
.
Finally, applying
(a,b)
-strong spatial mixing and noting that
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)\geq\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)-r
concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

Note that, without fixing a particular region
{\Lambda^{\prime}}\subseteq\Lambda
that contains
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}
in
Lemma

6.2
, we can always set
{\Lambda^{\prime}}=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\}
, which yields
\leq kv_{d}r^{d}
with
v_{d}
being the volume of a unit ball in
d
dimensions.

The following multiplicative bound for
k
-point densities with different activity functions follows immediately.

Corollary 6.4
.

Consider the setting of
Lemma

6.2
.
It holds that

\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+a\mathrm{e}^{br+\lambda}\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}}}\right)\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right).

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}
on
{\Lambda^{\prime}}
, it holds that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}
.
If
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}=0
, then the desired inequality holds trivially since both sides are
0
.

Assume
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}>0
.
Defining
f_{\boldsymbol{x}}
as in the proof of
Lemma

6.2
and following the same arguments we have

\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathds{E}_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}\circ\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}^{-1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f_{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}\right]=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathds{E}_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}\circ\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}^{-1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f_{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}\right].

Observe that
f_{\boldsymbol{x}}
is non-negative and
f_{\boldsymbol{x}}(\emptyset)=1
.
Combined with Poisson domination, we obtain

\mathds{E}_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}\circ\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}^{-1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{f_{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}\right]\geq\mu_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime},\Lambda}\circ\pi_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}}^{-1}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\emptyset}}\right\})\geq\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda},

which implies
\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\geq\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda}
.
In particular, we have
\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)>0
, and applying
Lemma

6.2
yields

\displaystyle\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)-\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}\right|}{\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}}\right)\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+a\mathrm{e}^{br+\lambda}\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}}}\right)\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right).\qed

6.2
Proof of
Lemma

6.1

To prove the main lemma of the section, we use the following identity by Michelen and Perkins
[
58
,
56
]
.

Lemma 6.5

(
[
58
, Lemma 12]
)
.

Let
{\Lambda^{\prime}}\subseteq\Lambda
be measurable.
Fix a point in
z\in{\Lambda^{\prime}}
and, for any given activity function
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
and any point
y\in\Lambda
, let

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{y}(w)=\begin{cases}0\text{ if }\widetilde{\mathrm{dist}}(z,w)<\widetilde{\mathrm{dist}}(z,y)\\
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)\text{ otherwise}\end{cases},

where
\widetilde{\mathrm{dist}}(u,v)=\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u,v}}\right)+diam({\Lambda^{\prime}})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{u,v}}\right\}\nsubseteq{\Lambda^{\prime}}}
for
u,v\in\mathds{R}^{d}
.
For all
k\in\mathds{N}
and
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{k}
, it holds that

\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus{\Lambda^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}=\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y}}}\right).

Moreover, we have

\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus{\Lambda^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}=\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y}}}\right).

We use
Corollary

6.4
to show the following intermediate statement.

Lemma 6.6
.

Consider the setting of
Lemma

6.2
.
For all
k\in\mathds{N}_{0}
and all
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{k}
it holds that

\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus{\Lambda^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}\leq\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({ae^{br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda+\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus{\Lambda^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)},

where
{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{(r)}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{y\in\Lambda\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \vphantom{y\in\Lambda}\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y,{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}\right)<r}}\right.}}\right\}
, and
Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
and
Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime})
are the partition functions on
\Lambda
for the potential
\phi
.

Proof.

Using
Lemma

6.5
we have

\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus{\Lambda^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}=\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y}}}\right),

where we treat case that
\boldsymbol{x}
is the empty tuple by setting
\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)=1
.
By
Corollary

6.4
, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+a\mathrm{e}^{br+\lambda}\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}}}\right)\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({a\mathrm{e}^{br+\lambda}\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}}}}\right)\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right).

Moreover, observe that
\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}-\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}}\right)\subseteq\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)
,
\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}\leq\boldsymbol{\lambda}<\lambda
and
\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}\leq\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}<\lambda
for all
y\in{\Lambda^{\prime}}
.
Thus,
Lemma

6.2
implies

\displaystyle-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y+\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)-\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y+a\mathrm{e}^{br}\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}\cdot\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}(y)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y+a\mathrm{e}^{br}\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}\cdot\lambda.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus{\Lambda^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({ae^{br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda+\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-b\cdot\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\Lambda^{\prime}},\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)}}}}\right)\rho_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({-\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\rho_{\widehat{(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}_{y}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\,\text{d}y}}}\right).

Applying
Lemma

6.5
again concludes the proof.
∎

With
Lemma

6.6
, we can prove
Lemma

6.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

6.1
.

We aim to apply
Lemma

6.6
.
To this end, we start by writing the involved partition functions in terms of two new activity functions
\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}
.

Set
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\lambda_{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda_{B}}
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}=\lambda_{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda_{B}}
, and observe that
\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}\leq\lambda
.
Set
k=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\boldsymbol{v}}}\right|
and let
\boldsymbol{x}\in\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}^{k}
be such that
\eta_{\boldsymbol{x}}=\eta\cap\boldsymbol{v}
(i.e., any tuple containing exactly the points in
\eta\cap\boldsymbol{v}
).
Note that

\lambda_{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap S)}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\eta\cap\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}

and analogously
\lambda_{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap S)}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}
.
Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}=\frac{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime})Z_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})Z_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}

(13)

We proceed by lower bounding the distance between
\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right)
and
\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}
.
Note that
\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))
and
\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))
agree on
\Lambda_{S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}
and can only disagree on
\Lambda_{H}
.
By construction, it holds that
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{H},\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}}\right)\geq\ell r
.
As the potential range is bounded by
r
, it follows that
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathrm{supp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\right),\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}}\right)\geq(\ell-1)r
.

Now, note that in particular
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}
agree on
\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}
.
We may assume
\lambda>0
, since otherwise all involved partition functions are
1
and the statement holds trivially.
Since further
\eta
is feasible we have
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}>0
.
Thus, multiplying (
13
) with
\frac{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}=1
yields

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime})Z_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\boldsymbol{x}})\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})Z_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime\boldsymbol{x}}\frac{Z_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{v}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})}{Z_{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime})}\mathrm{e}^{-H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)}}.

Finally, by
Lemma

6.6
we have the upper bound

\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi_{1}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\cdot\frac{Z_{B}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi_{2}\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\leq\mathrm{exp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({a3^{d}r^{d}\mathrm{e}^{2br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda r^{d}+\mathrm{e}^{\lambda 3^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-br\ell}}}}\right),

and applying the same reasoning after swapping the roles of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\prime}
results in the corresponding lower bound, which proves the claim.
∎

7
Hard-sphere model

In this section we focus on the hard-sphere model.
Recall that for an interaction range
r>0
, the hard-sphere model is defined by the potential

\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)=\begin{cases}\infty\text{ if }\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)<r\\
0\text{ otherwise }\end{cases}.

To simplify notation, define for every
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathds{R}^{k}
and
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathds{R}^{m}

\displaystyle D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i,j}}\right\}\in\binom{[k]}{2}}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},x_{j}}}\right)}=\prod_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{i,j}}\right\}\in\binom{[k]}{2}}\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},x_{j}}}\right)\geq r}\text{ and }

\displaystyle D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{j\in[m]}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},y_{j}}}\right)}=\prod_{i\in[k]}\prod_{j\in[m]}\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},y_{j}}}\right)\geq r}.

We extend this definition from tuples of points to finite point sets in the obvious way.
This allows us to write

Z_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}}^{\eta}(\lambda)=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{k}}D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\eta}}\right)\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x},

for all measurable
{\Lambda^{\prime}}\subseteq\Lambda
and all
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
.

7.1
Constructing the Bayes filter

We start by constructing a suitable Bayes filter correction for the hard-sphere model.
The key ingredient will be computing such a correction by enumerating a finite set of boundary configurations that closely approximates all possible boundary conditions.
This is made precise by the following lemma.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
be non-empty,
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
,
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
,
B=B\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\ell}}\right)
and
H=(S\cup B)^{c}
.
For all
\varepsilon>0
,
\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}
and

\delta\leq\varepsilon\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\xi\cap\partial B}}\right|2^{d}r^{d-1}d^{3/2}\lambda\mathrm{e}^{\lambda(2\ell+1)^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)^{-1}

there is some
\gamma\subseteq(\delta\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon}\cdot\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\leq\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}.

Proof.

Fix some
\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}
, let
\Phi
map every point in
\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
to its closest point in
(\delta\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
in
\ell_{\infty}
-distance and set
\gamma=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Phi(x)\mid x\in\xi\cap\partial B}}\right\}
.
We first prove that

\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\cdot Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}\leq Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\cdot Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}.

To this end, note that by
Lemma

C.2

Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}=Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{(\xi\cap\partial B)\cup(\eta\cap S)}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}-Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{(\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}})^{k}}D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid(\xi\cap\partial B)\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)-D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{(\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}})^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid(\xi\cap\partial B)\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)-D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}.

Next, observe that for every
\boldsymbol{x}\in(\Lambda_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}})^{k}
it holds that

D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid(\xi\cap\partial B)\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)\neq D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)

implies that there is some
i\in[k]
and some
y\in\xi\cap\partial B
such that either
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},y}}\right)\geq r>\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},\Phi(y)}}\right)
or
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},y}}\right)<r\leq\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},\Phi(y)}}\right)
.
Further, note that for every
y\in\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
(and particular
y\in\xi\cap\partial B
) it holds that
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y,\Phi(y)}}\right)\leq\sqrt{d}\delta
.
Using union bound and observing that
D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\cdot\mid(\xi\cap\partial B)\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)
and
D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\cdot\mid\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}\right)
are symmetric functions, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}-Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\xi\cap\partial B}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}-(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}}}\right]\cdot\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}k^{k-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\xi\cap\partial B}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}-(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}}}\right]\cdot\lambda\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}.

Elementary calculations yield

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}-(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}}}\right]\leq d^{3/2}\delta(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d-1}\leq d^{3/2}\delta 2^{d-1}r^{d-1}.

Further, it holds that

\leq\leq(2\ell+1)^{d}r^{d}.

Thus, for our choice of
\delta
we obtain

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}-Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}}\right|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

and, since

\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)},Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}}\right\}\geq 1,

this proves the desired multiplicative bound.

It remains to show

\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\cdot Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}\leq Z_{B}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\cdot Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))},

which is done analogously, concluding the proof.
∎

In particular, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
be non-empty,
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
,
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
,
B=B\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\ell}}\right)
and
H=(S\cup B)^{c}
.
For all
\varepsilon>0
and

\delta\leq\varepsilon\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4^{d}r^{-1}d^{(d+3)/2}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}\lambda\mathrm{e}^{\lambda(2\ell+1)^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)^{-1}

it holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon}\cdot\min_{\gamma\subseteq(\delta\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}}\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\leq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}\\
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)\text{ is feasible}\end{subarray}}\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}.

Proof.

Note that, if
\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}
is such that
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)
is feasible, this implies in particular that
\xi^{\prime}=\xi\cap\partial B
is feasible.
Thus, the claim follows from
Lemma

7.1
by arguing that every feasible configuration
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{H\cap\partial B}
satisfies
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\xi^{\prime}}}\right|\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\sqrt{d}}{r}}}\right)^{d}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}
.
To see this, note that every point in
\xi^{\prime}
blocks at least a volume of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{r}{2\sqrt{d}}}}\right)^{d}
, where no other point can be placed.
Moreover, it holds that

\leq\leq(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d},

which concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary

7.2
allows us to replace the minimization over the uncountable set of boundary conditions
\mathcal{N}_{H}
by a minimization over the finite set
(\delta\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
.
The second ingredient that we need for computing a suitable Bayes filter correction is a way to approximate the involved partition functions.

To this end, for every non-empty
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
and
\delta>0
, define

\displaystyle\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)=\sum_{\gamma\subseteq(\delta\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{S}}\lambda^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\gamma}}\right|}\delta^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\gamma}}\right|}\cdot D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma}}\right)\cdot D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right).

(14)

The follow lemma justifies using
\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)
as an approximation for the hard-sphere partition function
Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}}
, given that
\delta
is chosen sufficiently small.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
be non-empty and
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
.
For all
\varepsilon>0
and

\delta\leq\varepsilon\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[d^{3/2}2^{d}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{r^{-1},r^{d-1}}}\right\}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\lambda,\lambda^{2}}}\right\}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial S}}\right|+)\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}}}\right]^{-1}

it holds that

\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon}\cdot Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}}\leq\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon}\cdot Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}},

where
\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)
is defined as in (
14
).

Proof.

Define
\Lambda_{S,\delta}=\bigcup_{x\in\subseteq(\delta\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{S}}\mathds{B}^{(\infty)}_{\delta/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)
, where
\mathds{B}^{(\infty)}_{\delta/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)
is the closed
\delta/2
-ball around
x
in infinity norm.
Moreover, let
\Phi
map every point in
\Lambda_{S,\delta}
to its closest point in
(\delta\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{S}
in
\ell_{\infty}
-distance, breaking ties arbitrarily.
With some abuse of notation, we extend
\Phi
to tuples
\boldsymbol{x}\in\Lambda_{S,\delta}
by setting
\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})=(\Phi(x_{1}),\dots,\Phi(x_{k}))
.
Now, note that

\displaystyle\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\delta^{k}\sum_{\boldsymbol{x}\in(\delta\mathds{Z})^{dk}\cap\Lambda_{S}^{k}}D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\cdot D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}^{k}}D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})}}\right)\cdot D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x},

where the first equality uses the fact
D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)=0
whenever
\boldsymbol{x}
contains the same point more than once.

We proceed by relating
\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)
to
Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}}
in two steps.
First, we compare
Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}}
with
Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}
, and then we compare
Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}
with
\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)
using the expression above.

For the first part, note that by
Lemma

C.2
it holds that
Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}}=Z_{S}^{\eta\cap\partial S}
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle Z_{S}^{\eta\cap\partial S}

\displaystyle\leq Z_{\Lambda_{S}\setminus\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}\cdot Z_{\Lambda_{S}\cap\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}\cdot Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S},

where
\ominus
denotes the symmetric difference.
Analogously, it holds that

Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}\cdot Z_{S}^{\eta\cap\partial S}.

Thus, if we show that
\lambda\leq\varepsilon/2
for our choice of
\delta
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon/2}Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}}\leq Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon/2}Z_{S}^{\eta\cap S^{c}}.

(15)

To this end, note that, if
x\in\Lambda_{S,\delta}\setminus\Lambda_{S}
, then
x\notin\Lambda_{S}
but
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\Lambda_{S}}}\right)\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}\delta}{2}
.
Similarly, if
x\in\Lambda_{S}\setminus\Lambda_{S,\delta}
, then
x\in\Lambda_{S}
but
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\Lambda_{S}^{c}}}\right)\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}\delta}{2}
.
Taking the union bound over boxes
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
yields

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(r+\sqrt{d}\delta/2)^{d}-(r-\sqrt{d}\delta/2)^{d}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S}}\right|d^{3/2}\delta\cdot(r+\sqrt{d}\delta/2)^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq d^{3/2}2^{d-1}r^{-1}\delta.

Thus, for
\delta\leq\varepsilon\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda d^{3/2}2^{d}r^{-1}}}\right)^{-1}
the desired inequality is satisfied.

We proceed by relating
Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}
to
\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)
.
First, note that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}-\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\cdot D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)-D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})}}\right)\cdot D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)-D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)-D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}.

We bound each of the terms in this sum separately.
To this end, note that for any
\boldsymbol{x}\in\Lambda_{S,\delta}^{k}
it holds that
D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)\neq D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})}}\right)
implies that there are
i<j
such that either
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},x_{j}}}\right)<r\leq\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(x_{i}),\Phi(x_{j})}}\right)
or
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},x_{j}}}\right)\geq r>\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(x_{i}),\Phi(x_{j})}}\right)
.
Since
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\Phi(x)}}\right)\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}\delta}{2}
, applying union bound over
1\leq i<j\leq k
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}}}\right)-D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}-(r-\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d}}}\right]\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}k(k-1)^{k-1}

\displaystyle\leq d^{3/2}\delta\cdot(r+\sqrt{d}\delta)^{d-1}\lambda^{2}\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq d^{3/2}\delta 2^{d-1}r^{d-1}\lambda^{2}\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}\int_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{x}\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)-D\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})\mid\eta\cap\partial S}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq d^{3/2}\delta 2^{d-1}r^{d-1}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial S}}\right|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}.

Combining both and noting that
\Lambda_{S,\delta}\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in\Lambda_{S}}\mathds{B}^{(\infty)}_{\delta/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)\subseteq\Lambda_{S\cup\partial S}
yields

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}-\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)}}\right|\leq d^{3/2}\delta 2^{d-1}r^{d-1}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\lambda,\lambda^{2}}}\right\}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial S}}\right|+)\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}.

For
\delta\leq\varepsilon\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{3/2}2^{d}r^{d-1}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\lambda,\lambda^{2}}}\right\}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial S+}}\right|)\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}}}\right)^{-1}
this gives

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}-\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)}}\right|\leq\varepsilon/2,

and, since
Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}\geq 1
and
\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)\geq 1
,

\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon/2}\cdot Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}\leq\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\eta,\delta}}\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon/2}\cdot Z_{\Lambda_{S,\delta}}^{\eta\cap\partial S}.

Combining this with (
15
) concludes the proof.
∎

We now combine
Corollary

7.2
and
Lemma

7.3
to obtain our Bayes filter correction for the hard-sphere model.

Lemma 7.4
.

For
\varepsilon>0
, non-empty
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
and feasible
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
set

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\coloneqq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4^{d}r^{-1}d^{(d+3)/2}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}\lambda\mathrm{e}^{\lambda(2\ell+1)^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)^{-1}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\delta_{2}

\displaystyle=\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)\coloneqq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{3/2}2^{d}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{r^{-1},r^{d-1}}}\right\}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\lambda,\lambda^{2}}}\right\}m\mathrm{e}^{\lambda(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}}}}\right)^{-1},

where
m=2^{d}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\delta_{1}^{-d}+d^{d/2}r^{-d}}}\right)+(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}
, and define

C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta}}\right)\coloneqq\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon}\cdot\min_{\gamma\subseteq(\delta_{1}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}}\frac{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v},\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S),\delta_{2}}}\right)}{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B,\gamma\cup(\eta\cap\Lambda_{S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}),\delta_{2}}}\right)},

where
B=B\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\ell}}\right)
and
H=(S\cup B)^{c}
.
Then
C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta}}\right)
is a Bayes filter correction as in
Definition

4.1
.

Proof.

We start by arguing that
C_{\varepsilon}
is a Bayes filter correction.
For the measurability, note that for every fixed non-empty
S\subseteq\mathcal{V}
and
\boldsymbol{v}\in S
it holds that
C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\cdot}}\right)
is a minimum of a finite set of
\mathfrak{R}
-measurable functions.
Moreover, it can be easily seen that
C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta}}\right)=C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta^{\prime}}}\right)
for every two configurations
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}
that agree on
\Lambda_{S}
.

Next, we argue that for all feasible
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
it holds that
C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta}}\right)
is bounded away from
0
and

C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta}}\right)\leq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}\\
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)\text{ is feasible}\end{subarray}}\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}.

For the lower bound, note that for all
\gamma\subseteq(\delta_{1}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
it holds that

\frac{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v},\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S),\delta_{2}}}\right)}{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B,\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v})),\delta_{2}}}\right)}\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\lambda\delta_{2}}}\right)^{-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\delta_{2}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{B}}}\right|}>0

independent of
\eta
.
For the upper bound, we start by observing that, for our choice of
\delta_{1}
,
Corollary

7.2
yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon/2}\cdot\min_{\gamma\subseteq(\delta_{1}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}}\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\leq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}\\
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)\text{ is feasible}\end{subarray}}\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}.

Note that
\leq\leq(2\ell+1)^{d}r^{d}
and
\leq\leq(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}
.
Moreover, we have the crude bound
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\delta_{1}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}}}\right|\leq 2^{d}\delta_{1}^{-d}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}
, and, for every feasible
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
, it holds that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\Lambda_{S\cap\partial(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v})}}}\right|\leq 2^{d}d^{d/2}r^{-d}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}
.
Therefore, for all
\gamma\subseteq(\delta_{1}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S))\cap\partial(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v})}}\right|\leq 2^{d}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\delta_{1}^{-d}+d^{d/2}r^{-d}}}\right)
.
Analogously, it holds that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v})))\cap\partial B}}\right|\leq 2^{d}(2\ell+3)^{d}r^{d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\delta_{1}^{-d}+d^{d/2}r^{-d}}}\right)
.
Thus,
Lemma

7.3
yields for our choice of
\delta_{2}

\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon/2}\cdot\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}\leq\frac{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v},\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S),\delta_{2}}}\right)}{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B,\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v})),\delta_{2}}}\right)}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon/2}\cdot\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\gamma\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}}.

In particular, this proves

\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon}\cdot\min_{\gamma\subseteq(\delta_{1}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}}\frac{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v},\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S),\delta_{2}}}\right)}{\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B,\gamma\cup(\eta\cap\Lambda_{S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}),\delta_{2}}}\right)}\leq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{H}\\
\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)\text{ is feasible}\end{subarray}}\frac{Z_{B\setminus\boldsymbol{v}}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap S)}}{Z_{B}^{\xi\cup(\eta\cap(S\setminus\boldsymbol{v}))}},

implying that
C_{\varepsilon}
is a Bayes filter correction.
∎

7.2
Efficiency of the algorithm

We now argue that under the assumption of strong spatial mixing we can use
C_{\varepsilon}
to obtain an efficient implementation of
Algorithm

1
.
Our argument will consist of two steps.
First, we discuss how to implement each step of the algorithm efficiently.
In particular, we argue that we can efficiently update the configuration (line
1
), and that we can efficiently sample a Bayes filter based on
C_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
(line
1
).
For the latter, we make use of a Bernoulli factory to circumvent the lack of an algorithm for exact computation of partition functions.
In the second part, we focus on the overall number of iterations of the algorithm.
This is where the assumption of strong spatial mixing comes into play to ensure that the success probability of our Bayes filter is sufficiently large, which implies rapid termination of the algorithm.

We start with discussing the running time of each iteration of
Algorithm

1
.
For updating the configuration, we will use a rejection sampling method which, as long as the updated region
\Lambda_{B}
has constant volume, will be efficient enough for our setting.
Since we apply the same argument for more general repulsive potentials, the following lemma is stated in this general setting.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
B\subseteq\mathcal{V}
and
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
.
For any repulsive finite-range potential
\phi
we can sample from
\mu_{B}^{\eta\cap(B)^{c}}
in expected time
(\lambda+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial B}}\right|)\cdot\lambda\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Let
P_{B,\lambda}
denote a Poisson point process on
\Lambda_{B}
with intensity
\lambda
.
We consider the rejection sampling algorithm given in
Algorithm

2
.

repeat

1

Draw
Y\sim P_{B,\lambda}

Compute
w=\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x,y}}\right\}\in\binom{Y}{2}}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{x\in\eta\cap\partial B,y\in Y}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)}

Draw
W\sim\mathrm{Ber}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)

until

W=1
;

2

return

Y

Algorithm 2

Sample from
\mu_{B}^{\eta\cap(B)^{c}}

To prove that this rejection sampling method produces the correct out put distribution, it suffices to argue that
w
as computed in the algorithm is proportional to the density of
\mu_{B}^{\eta\cap(B)^{c}}
with respect to
P_{B,\lambda}
for
P_{B,\lambda}
-almost all
\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{B}
.
This is true since for all
\xi\in\mathcal{N}_{B}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\eta\cap B^{c},y\in\xi}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\eta\cap\partial B,y\in\xi}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)+\sum_{x\in\eta\cap(B\cup\partial B)^{c},y\in\xi}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\eta\cap\partial B,y\in\xi}\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right),

where the last equality follows from the fact that
\mathrm{\mathrm{dist}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{B},(\Lambda_{B\cup\partial B})^{c}}}\right)\geq r
and therefore
\phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y}}\right)=0
for all
x\in(\Lambda_{B\cup\partial B})^{c},y\in\Lambda_{B}
.

We proceed by using Wald’s identity as given in
Lemma

B.1
to bound the expected running time of the procedure above.
To this end, let
(Y_{n})_{n\in\mathds{N}}
be a sequence of independent samples from
P_{B,\lambda}
.
Assume the algorithm draws
Y=Y_{n}
at iteration
n\in\mathds{N}
.
Let
S_{n}
denote the running time of the rejection sampler in iteration
n
and let
N
be the (random) number of iterations until the algorithm terminates.
We aim for bounding
\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sum_{n=1}^{N}S_{n}}}}\right]
.

First, observe that
S_{n}
is dominated by the time for computing
w
, implying
S_{n}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Y_{n}}}\right|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Y_{n}}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap B}}\right|
.
Since further
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Y_{n}}}\right|
follows a Poisson distribution with parameter
\lambda
, we have
\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{S_{n}}}}\right]\leq\lambda^{2}{}^{2}+\lambda\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial B}}\right|=(\lambda+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial B}}\right|)\cdot\lambda
.

Moreover, observe that the random variable
\mathds{1}_{N\geq n}
only depends on
(Y_{i})_{i\leq n-1}
, whereas
S_{n}
only depends on
Y_{n}
.
Therefore,
S_{n}
and
\mathds{1}_{N\geq n}
are independent and
\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{S_{n}\mathds{1}_{N\geq n}}}}\right]=\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{S_{n}}}}\right]\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{N\geq n}}}}\right]
.

Applying
Lemma

B.1
yields
\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sum_{n=1}^{N}S_{n}}}}\right]\leq(\lambda+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial B}}\right|)\cdot\lambda\cdot\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{N}}}\right]
.
To obtain a bound
\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{N}}}\right]
on, observe that the algorithm always terminates if
Y=\emptyset
, which happens in every iteration independently with a probability of
\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda}
.
Thus,
N
is dominated by a geometric random variable with success probability
\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda}
and
\mathds{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{N}}}\right]\leq\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Note that, in the case of the hard-sphere model with
r>0
, if
\eta\in\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda}
is feasible, then
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta\cap\partial B}}\right|
is a linear function of the volume .

We proceed with bounding the running time for sampling the Bayes filter for the hard-sphere model in each step.
To this end, we start with the following observation.

Observation 7.6
.

Consider the setting of
Lemma

7.4
.
The required running time for computing
C_{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S,\boldsymbol{v},\eta}}\right)
does only depend on
\varepsilon
,
\ell
,
r
,
\lambda
and
d
.

This follows directly from enumerating all subsets
\gamma\subseteq(\delta_{1}\mathds{Z})^{d}\cap\Lambda_{H\cap\partial B}
and brute-force computation of
\hat{Z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B\setminus\boldsymbol{v},\gamma\cup(\eta\cap S),\delta_{2}}}\right)
and
